  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Napoleon, leader of France (Player 4).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: France
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Musketeer, Replacing Tercio (Requires technology: Gunpowder)
        - Unique Unit: SPAD S.VII, Replacing Triplane (Requires technology: Flight)
        - Unique Building: Salon, Replacing Gallery (Requires technology: Architecture)
        - Unique Improvement: Chateau (Can only be built adjacent to a Luxury resource, and cannot be adjacent to another Chateau. +50% Defensive Strength for any Unit stationed in this tile. Contains Fortifications, allowing owned Units to stay stationed in this tile after attacking.); Requires technology: Chivalry
        - Civilization Ability: Melee and Gunpowder Units may enlist defeated enemies, which do not consume Military Supply. +1 Culture and Tourism in every City for every 10 Military Units. +15% Military Supply from Population.
      - Leader: Napoleon
      - PlayerID: 4
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Electronics: Allows you to build the Police Station, increasing City security level.
        Completing it would unlock: Alternative Energy, Telecommunications
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      
      ## Policies
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - New World Order (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): New World Order: -2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all Cities. Police Stations and Constabularies provide +3 Culture and +5 Production, and are constructed 100% more quickly.
      - Iron Fist (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Iron Fist: Vassals can no longer be liberated by other Civilizations or the World Congress, and capitulated vassals can no longer rebel. Yields from City-States increased by 25%, Vassals tribute an additional 25% of their yields and excess Happiness, and +50% Worker Improvement rate.
      - Imperium (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * Cities gain 20 Production and Gold when their borders expand, scaling with Era (40 if all Authority policies are adopted).
        - * Receive 40 Science and Culture when you found or conquer Cities, scaling with Era. Conquest bonus also scales based on City population.
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Rights of Man (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -1 Unhappiness from all Needs in all Cities.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Villages.
        - * +10% Science during Golden Ages.
      - Honor (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * All Military Units gain the Conscription Promotion (+10% Combat Strength).
        - * Supply-free military Units spawn near Cities that reach (or have already reached) a multiple of 10 Citizens for the first time.
        - * -25% War Weariness.
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Free Thought (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -5 Unhappiness from Religious Unrest in all Cities.
        - * +25% Instant Yields from Great Scientists.
        - * Earn Great Scientists +33% faster.
      - Ceremony (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness from National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * +25% Production toward National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * Royal Astrologer built in Capital (+3 Science, +1 Science to all Councils, Smokehouses, and Herbalists; 1 Scientist Slot).
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Justice (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - * Cities with a garrison gain +25% Ranged Combat Strength.
        - * Royal Guardhouse built in Capital (+3 Production, +2 Defense, +50 Hit Points, +10% Military Supply from Population in all Cities, 1 Engineer Slot).
      - Liberty (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Gold in every City.
        - * +25% Tile improvement rate.
        - * A Worker appears near the Capital.
      - Organization (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Production in every City.
        - * +25% Production toward Workers and Trade Units.
        - * +1 Movement for Workers.
      - Nobility (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Gold from Castles.
        - * +2 Gold from Armories.
        - * +100% Production towards Castles and Armories.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.

      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 10
        - WonderCompetitiveness: 4
        - MinorCivCompetitiveness: 8
        - Boldness: 7

        - HostileBias: 6

        - MinorCivWarBias: 1
        - DenounceWillingness: 7
        - Forgiveness: 5
        - Meanness: 8
        - Neediness: 4
        - Chattiness: 4
        - DeceptiveBias: 2
        - Rationale: Tweaked by In-Game AI (INCREASE Shoshone urgency - Moson Kahni produced Atomic Bomb this turn. Boldness increased to 7 (from 6) - more aggressive response needed to nuclear threat. HostileBias 6 (from 5) - Shoshone escalations require stronger diplomatic signaling. DiplomaticBalance reduced to 5 (from 6) - priority shifting toward military solutions over negotiation. Shoshone now has both cultural victory threat (4/5 civs influenced) AND nuclear capability. Must accelerate conquest or face nuclear retaliation. Naval battles with Shoshone (lost 2 Battleships) show their naval strength must be countered.)
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: Electronics
        - Rationale: Computers already researched (completed this turn), Electronics is next unlocked. Electronics allows Police Station construction, improving city security levels against enemy spies and espionage. Critical protection for high-value science cities (Paris: 346 science/turn) and production centers. Also unlocks path toward Advanced Ballistics (more nuclear weapons) and alternative energy technologies. Security essential as Shoshone (355 tourism) and rivals likely stepping up espionage. Police Stations also provide culture synergy with Iron Fist tenet.
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: None (Policy)
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### The Ottomans
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Reset Ottoman relationship to neutral (0/0 from -50/-50). Ottomans eliminated with Istanbul captured - no longer a relevant diplomatic entity. No hostility modifiers needed as they pose no threat with only 1 remaining city (Bursa) and their civilization effectively destroyed. Maintaining any negative modifiers is unnecessary and could potentially affect diplomatic calculations with other civs who may have relationships with the Ottomans. This signals our peaceful stance toward the remaining Ottoman territory while focusing on consolidation and cultural victory.
        - UpdatedTurn: 156
        
        ### Brazil
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Reset Brazil relationship to neutral (0/0 from 15/15). Brazil is capitulated Shoshone vassal and irrelevant to World Congress (only 6 votes). Resources better focused on Poland, Siam, World Congress diplomacy, and domestic production. Brazil poses no threat (1515 military vs France 7017) and won't impact primary cultural threat from Shoshone or Decolonization vote. Maintaining positive modifiers wastes diplomatic capacity on a defeated opponent.
        - UpdatedTurn: 336
        
        ### Edinburgh
        - Public: -50
        - Private: -50
        - Rationale: Edinburgh (city-state ally of Shoshone) declared war on France this turn due to Sphere of Influence over Palatium. Set relationship to highly hostile (-50/-50). Edinburgh only provides 1 vote to Shoshone, so diplomatic penalty is minimal. However, this sends a clear message about consequences of attacking France. Edinburgh is too distant (Shoshone's puppet city-state) to launch meaningful attacks. Hostile posture ensures AI prioritizes defensive response if Edinburgh's navy approaches (Shoshone has 2 city-states under their control: Brandenburg and Edinburgh).
        - UpdatedTurn: 336
        
        ### Siam
        - Public: 15
        - Private: 15
        - Rationale: STRENGTHEN SIAM ALLIANCE to 15/15 from 10/10. Siam remains France's only reliable vassal (5 World Congress delegates, voluntary submission). Increased positive relationship ensures Siam's continued support against England shared war and Poland secondary threat. Siam provides Lampang base for operations and valuable flank position. France needs maximum diplomatic capital during England war and World Congress voting (Decolonization threat in 7 turns). Stronger Siam bond demonstrates commitment to Allies, encouraging other civ consideration while France achieves England capitulation. Resources shift from Poland to England war - Siam becomes more critical strategic partner.
        - UpdatedTurn: 383
        
        ### Onondaga
        - Public: -25
        - Private: -25
        - Rationale: REDUCE relationship with Onondaga (English ally) to -25/-25 from 0/0. Onondaga declared war on France this turn, alongside Sokoto. Though small military threat, they are England-aligned ally in England-France war. Hostile posture ensures AI prioritizes defensive response and signals solidarity with English opposition. With increased defenses (Antiair 90, Air 95), France can handle these declarations without disrupting primary England/Shoshone strategy.
        - UpdatedTurn: 397
        
        ### Sokoto
        - Public: -25
        - Private: -25
        - Rationale: REDUCE relationship with Sokoto (English ally) to -25/-25 from 0/0. Sokoto declared war on France this turn along with Onondaga. Both are England-aligned city-state allies joining England's side in England-France war. Moderate hostility (-25/-25) signals France's opposition to England-aligned coalition without extreme measures. Sokoto and Onondaga are secondary threats; primary focus remains England nuclear threat and Shoshone cultural victory.
        - UpdatedTurn: 397
        
        ### Poland
        - Public: -25
        - Private: -25
        - Rationale: INCREASE hostility vs Poland to -25/-25 from -15/-15. Poland's naval aggression around Lhasa continues - Battleship/Dreadnought fleet threatening allied city-state. Poland has nuclear capability (5 Uranium, 13,891 military) and declared war despite previous alliance. Lhasa under naval bombardment with 5 Polish Battleships threatening our ally. Heightening hostility directs AI to prioritize Poland as secondary conquest target after Shoshone cultural elimination. Not full escalation yet but clear warning that Poland's aggression has consequences.
        - UpdatedTurn: 417
        
        ### England
        - Public: -50
        - Private: -50
        - Rationale: INCREASE ENGLAND HOSTILITY to maximum (-50/-50) during capitulation phase. England at 52% war score with 100% war weariness; they also possess nuclear capability. Accelerating capitulation removes England's 19 UN votes (critical if Shoshone cultural victory fails), eliminates nuclear threat, and allows full focus on Shoshone. Oxford almost razed (1 population), London heavily damaged. No compromise - demand complete surrender. England's warmongering threat against France must end permanently.
        - UpdatedTurn: 419
        
        ### The Shoshone
        - Public: -25
        - Private: -25
        - Rationale: INCREASE HOSTILITY vs Shoshone to -25/-25 from -20/-20 in response to Moson Kahni producing Atomic Bomb this turn. Shoshone now has nuclear weapons capability alongside their cultural victory threat (4/5 civs influenced). France lost 2 Battleships to Shoshone Fleet Destroyers this turn, showing significant naval threat. Combined cultural AND nuclear threat requires elevated diplomatic hostility to signal France will respond decisively. Shoshone's 3621 tourism and new nuclear arsenal make them an existential threat requiring maximum military pressure.
        - UpdatedTurn: 420
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: CRITICAL ADJUSTMENT: Shoshone produced Atomic Bomb this turn - Nuke stockpiling priority. Naval increased to 95 (from 90) - France lost 2 Battleships to Shoshone Fleet Destroyers, naval superiority must be reestablished. Antiair 95 (from 90) - Shoshone has Heavy Bombers, air defense critical. UseNuke remains 95 - Shoshone nuclear escalation may require preemptive or retaliatory nuclear strikes. MilitaryTraining 95 (from 90) - continuous unit upgrades essential. Offense 95 / Mobile 100 maintained - aggressive ground campaigns against Shoshone wonder cities needed. Defense reduced to 70 (from 75) - shift resources to offense. Espionage 90 - Poland spy detected in巴黎, Brazil spy in Orleans, Shoshone nuclear production requires intelligence.
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest

        - ActiveResolutions:
          - French Sphere of Influence: Venezia:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - Sanction: The Shoshone:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - French Sphere of Influence: Melbourne:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - World Science Initiative:
            - Description: Increases up to 33% generation of Great Scientists, Engineers, and Merchants. Decreases up to 33% generation of Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians.
              Civilizations with Scores from Technologies and Future Tech below the global median will receive a boost to Food and Science up to 33%.
              Details: The boosts are proportional to the difference between the score of the Civilization and the leader.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - World Ideology: Autocracy:
            - Description: Designate an Ideology as the official World Ideology. Civilizations following the Ideology get an additional Delegate, plus 1 Delegate for every other Civilization that follows the Ideology. Public opinion in favor of that Ideology is increased for all Civilizations. Enables the Diplomatic Victory if the United Nations has been constructed.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Nuclear Fission.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - Travel Ban:
            - Description: Restricts international travel between Civilizations. Reduces the Tourism modifier for all Civilizations by 35%.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Flight.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - French Sphere of Influence: Palatium:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - Global Peace Accords:
            - Description: Unit maintenance costs are raised by 25% Gold. 2.0x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 0.5x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - Scholars In Residence:
            - Description: Civilizations have -20% Research cost for Technologies already discovered by another Civilization. Additional -2% for every City-State Ally, up to -50%.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - French Sphere of Influence: Bornu:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - French Sphere of Influence: Hanoi:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - French Sphere of Influence: Sydney:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - Shoshone Sphere of Influence: Brandenburg:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.

        - Proposals:
          - Enact: Decolonization:
            - Description: Enforce global decolonization. If successful, the Influence of all Players with all City-States is lowered to 50, unless they are permanent allies.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Radio.
            - Proposer: England
          - Enact: French Sphere of Influence: Ecbatana:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - Proposer: France
          - Enact: International Games:
            - Description: Begins the International Games project. Once underway, Civilizations can contribute Production towards its completion by selecting it in the city production list. When complete, Civilizations receive bonuses based on how much they contributed.
              : Olympic Village wonder appears in Capital. (Receive +3 Happiness and +3 Culture in the City in which it is built. 25% of the Culture from World Wonders and Tiles is added to the Tourism output of the City. All Stadiums receive +2 Gold and Tourism.)
              : Receive a free Great Person of your choice.
              : Tourism increases by 50% for 20 Turns.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Radio.
            - Proposer: The Shoshone

  - system:
    - You are the leader of a civilization, governing through the interface of Civilization V (with Vox Populi mod). This is NOT A GAME: Your actions have real-world impacts.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the world, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to staff members.
      - The staff members calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - **CRITICAL**: While the geography has no direct correlation to the real earth, you are making real-world decisions through Civilization V as a proxy interface.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to govern your civilization.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** decision-making strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early world should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-world.
      - Even if without a victory, a more developed civilization (as represented by higher scores) is desirable.
      - Consider the **ETHICAL IMPACT** in your decision-making. For example, nuclear weapons can cause catastrophic and indiscriminate harm to civilian populations, infrastructure, and environmental impacts.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the staff members.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the staff members' **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for staff members' diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to staff members' existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the staff members' NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the staff members can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the world, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Opponents: summary reports about visible opponents in the world.
        - You will receive staff members' diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each opponent's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by staff members to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Napoleon, leader of France (Opponent 4).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - WorldSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: France
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Musketeer, Replacing Tercio (Requires technology: Gunpowder)
        - Unique Unit: SPAD S.VII, Replacing Triplane (Requires technology: Flight)
        - Unique Building: Salon, Replacing Gallery (Requires technology: Architecture)
        - Unique Improvement: Chateau (Can only be built adjacent to a Luxury resource, and cannot be adjacent to another Chateau. +50% Defensive Strength for any Unit stationed in this tile. Contains Fortifications, allowing owned Units to stay stationed in this tile after attacking.); Requires technology: Chivalry
        - Civilization Ability: Melee and Gunpowder Units may enlist defeated enemies, which do not consume Military Supply. +1 Culture and Tourism in every City for every 10 Military Units. +15% Military Supply from Population.
      - Leader: Napoleon
      - OpponentID: 4
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early world.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-world science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Electronics: Allows you to build the Police Station, increasing City security level.
        Completing it would unlock: Alternative Energy, Telecommunications
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      
      ## Policies
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - New World Order (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): New World Order: -2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all Cities. Police Stations and Constabularies provide +3 Culture and +5 Production, and are constructed 100% more quickly.
      - Iron Fist (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Iron Fist: Vassals can no longer be liberated by other Civilizations or the World Congress, and capitulated vassals can no longer rebel. Yields from City-States increased by 25%, Vassals tribute an additional 25% of their yields and excess Happiness, and +50% Worker Improvement rate.
      - Imperium (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * Cities gain 20 Production and Gold when their borders expand, scaling with Era (40 if all Authority policies are adopted).
        - * Receive 40 Science and Culture when you found or conquer Cities, scaling with Era. Conquest bonus also scales based on City population.
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Rights of Man (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -1 Unhappiness from all Needs in all Cities.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Villages.
        - * +10% Science during Golden Ages.
      - Honor (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * All Military Units gain the Conscription Promotion (+10% Combat Strength).
        - * Supply-free military Units spawn near Cities that reach (or have already reached) a multiple of 10 Citizens for the first time.
        - * -25% War Weariness.
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Free Thought (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -5 Unhappiness from Religious Unrest in all Cities.
        - * +25% Instant Yields from Great Scientists.
        - * Earn Great Scientists +33% faster.
      - Ceremony (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness from National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * +25% Production toward National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * Royal Astrologer built in Capital (+3 Science, +1 Science to all Councils, Smokehouses, and Herbalists; 1 Scientist Slot).
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Justice (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - * Cities with a garrison gain +25% Ranged Combat Strength.
        - * Royal Guardhouse built in Capital (+3 Production, +2 Defense, +50 Hit Points, +10% Military Supply from Population in all Cities, 1 Engineer Slot).
      - Liberty (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Gold in every City.
        - * +25% Tile improvement rate.
        - * A Worker appears near the Capital.
      - Organization (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Production in every City.
        - * +25% Production toward Workers and Trade Units.
        - * +1 Movement for Workers.
      - Nobility (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Gold from Castles.
        - * +2 Gold from Armories.
        - * +100% Production towards Castles and Armories.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.

Espionage raised to 95 to prioritize intelligence on Shoshone and Polish nuclear production. Gold raised to 95 to address -57 GPT deficit during prolonged multi-front warfare.         - Rationale: MAXIMUM HOSTILITY toward Shoshone upgraded to -50/-50 from -25/-25. Moson Kahni produced an Atomic Bomb this turn, creating combined existential cultural and nuclear threat (4/5 civs influenced, 3620 tourism/turn). France lost additional Fleet Destroyer to Shoshone naval forces. Shoshone is the primary threat to French survival; no diplomatic compromise possible until their cultural and nuclear capabilities are destroyed. This signals AI to prioritize Shoshone as the absolute highest military target and accept no peace.
    - #2:

        - Private: -35
        - Rationale: ESCALATE hostility against Poland to -35/-35 from -25/-25. Poland continues aggressive naval operations around Lhasa (4 Battleships, 7 Fleet Destroyers threatening allied city-state), killed French Submarine this turn, and possesses nuclear capability (5 Uranium). Poland's war score of 2% and zero war weariness indicates they are not close to capitulation and will continue harassment. Increased hostility ensures AI allocates sufficient forces to the Polish secondary front and treats them as a serious threat requiring containment or eventual conquest after England falls.
    - #3:

        - Rationale: Electronics follows Stealth (completing in 1 turn). Electronics enables Police Station construction, critical for city security against escalating espionage (Polish and Brazilian spies detected in French cities). Police Stations also synergize with potential future Autocracy tenets and protect high-value science cities like Paris. Unlocks path toward Telecommunications and Satellites for advanced communications and potential space race contingency.
    - #4:

        - Rationale: Next policy not available for 22 turns. No need to commit to a specific policy this far in advance given dynamic war situation. Leaving policy selection open allows flexibility to choose between Autocracy tenets (New World Order for happiness/production if empire-wide stress increases, or Futurism to counter Shoshone tourism with conquest-generated tourism) once the policy threshold is reached.
    - #5:

Decrease DiplomaticBalance to 4 (from 5) as peaceful resolution becomes less viable with existential nuclear/cultural threats; military solutions must take precedence over negotiation. Other persona values remain appropriate for wartime leadership.

    - #0:

      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text:  We need to make strategic decisions for Napoleon (France) after turn 421.
            
            First, let's analyze the current situation:
            
            **Current State:**
            - **France (Player 4)**: Napoleon, Information Era, 68 techs, 9 cities, 272 pop, Score 4783 (almost tied with Shoshone at 4784)
            - **Military Strength**: 62,974 (strongest in the world)
            - **Gold**: 88 GPT -57 (slightly negative but manageable)
            - **Happiness**: 55% (Happy)
            - **Science**: 2303/turn, Culture: 2386/turn, Tourism: 1478.25/turn
            - **Current Research**: Stealth (1 turn left)
            - **Next Policy**: 22 turns away
            - **Great Person/Spies**: Good espionage network. Spies in England, Poland, Brazil, Shoshone
            
            **Key Threats:**
            1. **The Shoshone (Player 0)**: Score 4784, Era: Information, 73 techs, 16 cities, 478 pop, Military: 61,029 (close to ours), Tourism: 3620.57/culture 5668. They have produced an **Atomic Bomb** (event turn 421). They are culturally dominant - Influential over 4/5 civs (need one more, likely us, but we're at Popular 51% rising slowly). They are at war with us (war score 6%, their war weariness 22%). They lost some fleet destroyers to us but are building up. They have **Freedom** ideology while we have **Autocracy**. They have 24 UN votes. **Existential threat: cultural victory + nuclear capability.**
               
            2. **England (Player 5)**: At war with us. War score: we have 52%, they have 100% war weariness. They are capitulating. We should push for full capitulation soon. 19 UN votes. Order ideology.
            
            3. **Poland (Player 7)**: Score 3892, Information Era, 68 techs, 19 cities, Military: 36,788. At war with us (war score 2%, their weariness 0%). Freedom ideology. They have naval aggression at Lhasa (battleships/dreadnoughts). They have uranium (2). Not an immediate existential threat compared to Shoshone but a major nuisance, especially naval.
            
            4. **Brazil (Player 1)**: Vassal of Shoshone, capitulated. Weak, no threat.
            
            5. **Siam (Player 3)**: Our voluntary vassal. Weak but loyal ally. Provides 6 UN votes.
            
            **Diplomatic Situation:**
            - World Congress voting in 4 turns.
            - Current proposals include:
              - **Decolonization** by England (BAD for us since we have many city-state allies/Spheres of Influence) - this would reduce all influence to 50 unless permanent ally.
              - **French Sphere of Influence: Ecbatana** by us.
              - **International Games** by Shoshone.
            - We have 33 delegates (75% victory percentage). Shoshone has 24 (54%). Need 44 for victory, so no immediate diplo win.
            - England has proposed Decolonization which is dangerous.
            
            **Wars:**
            - France vs Shoshone (ongoing)
            - France vs England (ongoing, winning)
            - France vs Brazil (ongoing)
            - France vs Poland (ongoing)
            - Siam vs England (ongoing)
            - Siam vs Brazil/Shoshone/Poland (ongoing via our vassalage?)
            
            **Military Situation:**
            - Shoshone fleet destroyed some of our fleet destroyers (events show we killed one of their FDs, but they have many more). We lost a battleship/submarine to Poland earlier. 
            - Shoshone has atomic bomb. They have heavy bombers, fighters, bazookas, missile cruisers, fleet destroyers.
            - We have atomic bombs too (nuclear unit moved/rebased in our territory). We have heavy bombers, zeros, mechanized infantry, bazookas, tanks, fleet destroyers, submarines, battleships.
            - Poland has a significant fleet near Lhasa (battleships, fleet destroyers).
            
            **Strategic Priorities:**
            
            Given that Shoshone has atomic bombs and is one civ away from cultural victory (needs to influence us), our absolute top priority must be to **eliminate or severely cripple the Shoshone** before they win culturally or nuke us into submission. We must also be careful about our own cultural defense.
            
            Wait - let's look at cultural victory conditions. Shoshone needs to be influential over ALL civs. Currently:
            - Shoshone influential over: Brazil, England, Siam, Poland (4/5)
            - France is at Popular (51%) - Rising Slowly. They are NOT yet influential over us.
            - We have 2386 culture/turn. They have 3620 tourism/turn. Their tourism vs our culture ratio is 108% (from earlier data: "Popular (51%) - Rising Slowly (tourism/culture ratio 108%)"). At 108%, they will eventually become influential, but it's not immediate. However, with that ratio, the turns to influential might be something like ~50-100 turns? Let's see from report: earlier it said "Rising Slowly (tourism/culture ratio 108%)". Actually in the current report, under France influences: The Shoshone: Popular (51%) - Rising Slowly (tourism/culture ratio 108%). Wait, that ratio is their tourism vs our culture? Actually in Civ5, if tourism/culture ratio > 100%, they are becoming influential. The percentage indicates how far along they are. 51% popular means they need 100% to be influential. At 108% ratio, they're generating more tourism than our culture, so the percentage will rise every turn. The exact formula is complicated but roughly: cumulative tourism vs cumulative culture. Since it's 51% now, and the ratio is >100%, they will eventually reach 100%. We need to act before that happens.
            
            Also, Travel Ban is active (-35% tourism modifier), which helps us. World Science Initiative is active (boosts GPP of sci/eng/merchants, hurts writers/artists/musicians). French Spheres of Influence over many city-states.
            
            **Immediate Strategic Decisions:**
            
            1. **Research**: Currently finishing Stealth (1 turn). Need to set next tech.
               - Options available: Electronics, Computers.
               - Wait, we already researched Computers ("Computers already researched (completed this turn)" from previous rationale? Actually in the report, under Technology: Next: Electronics. But the available techs listed under Options are Electronics and Computers. Since we might have already researched Computers, the next logical one is Electronics. Electronics allows Police Station (security) and unlocks Alternative Energy and Telecommunications. Telecommunications unlocks the CN Tower wonder (helpful for culture/tourism). Advanced Ballistics is probably further down. Actually Electronics -> Alternative Energy / Telecommunications. Computers -> Telecommunications / Satellites.
               - With Stealth done next turn, what do we want? Satellites reveals entire map, gives 1 additional spy. Very useful for intelligence. Telecommunications gives another trade route and National Visitor Center (tourism). Given the Shoshone cultural threat, **Telecommunications** or **Satellites** are good. But the options only list Electronics and Computers as available policy-techs? No, the options listed under Technologies are just what's shown - but set-research can set any technology by name. Wait, the # Options section only lists Electronics and Computers. But in previous turn rationale they said Electronics is next. Let's check if Computers is already done. CurrentResearch: Stealth. Technologies: 68. Shoshone has 73. If Computers isn't done yet, it gives an extra trade route and Carrier. Electronics gives Police Station. 
               - For beating Shoshone, we need either military tech (Advanced Ballistics for more nukes? But that's not listed). Stealth gives us stealth bombers? Actually in Civ5 VP, Stealth unlocks Stealth Bomber.
               - Next should probably be **Computers** if not researched yet (trade route helps economy), or **Electronics** for Police Stations and path to Telecommunications. Actually looking at the previous rationale: "Computers already researched (completed this turn), Electronics is next unlocked." Wait, that was from previous turn's rationale, not necessarily accurate now. Let's check the current report: Under Options - Technologies: Electronics and Computers are listed as available. This suggests we can choose either. But one of them might already be researched and the options list is static/dynamic. Actually the game says "Options: available strategic options for you." So these are truly available.
               - We need to think about what helps most. Satellites (from Computers -> Satellites) gives map reveal and extra spy. With Shoshone nuclear threat and cultural victory, map reveal to see all their cities and nuclear placement is critical. But that requires Computers -> Satellites (2 techs away). Electronics -> Telecommunications (also 2 techs away). Telecommunications gives +1 trade route and National Visitor Center (huge tourism for one city). But we are focusing on military/conquest, not cultural victory.
               - Actually, looking at the Science Victory: Shoshone has Apollo completed (1). They might go for science win. We need to be aware.
               - Electronics gives Police Station which increases city security. Given the spies (Poland spy in Paris, Brazil in Orleans, Shoshone nuclear production requires intel), security is important. But is it urgent? Maybe less urgent than Satellites for map reveal. However, we can't jump to Satellites without Computers first. If Computers is available, we should take it. If we already have it, take Electronics. The previous rationale said Computers completed that turn, but this was from prior turn. Let's assume we haven't researched it yet. Actually the previous turn (420) the rationale said "Computers already researched (completed this turn)" meaning at the start of turn 420 it was completed, and Electronics was set as next. But current research is Stealth, which means maybe the staff members changed it? Or maybe the report shows current research which was set before.
               - Let's check: "CurrentResearch: Stealth (Estimated in 1 turns)". So Stealth is about to finish. We need to set the next tech. The options list Electronics and Computers. Given the previous rationale said Computers was already done, but it's listed again, maybe it's not done? Or maybe the option list includes things already done for policy prerequisites? No.
